[services] services/stage-design (id 46)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE), _status=draft (admin-only draft state)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Stage design is the architecture of attention — and as a stage design company based in Portland, Oregon, Beatrox designs, engineers, and builds stages for productions nationwide. It's the physical and visual environment that frames a performance, keynote, or presentation — the structure, surfaces, lighting positions, and sightlines that determine what every person in the room sees and feels. A great stage doesn't just hold the show; it becomes part of the story.\n\nYou need stage design when a rental deck and a drape line won't carry the concept. A product launch that needs the reveal built into the architecture, a touring artist whose set piece has to fly in any orientation, a conference stage that has to read on camera as well as it reads in the room — these are design problems, not equipment problems.\n\nDone well, stage design disappears into the experience. The audience never thinks about load ratings or sightline studies — they just know the moment felt massive. That takes a team that designs, engineers, and builds under one roof."
  AFTER:  "Stage design is the architecture of attention, and as a stage design company based in Portland, Oregon, Beatrox designs, engineers, and builds stages for productions nationwide. It's the physical and visual environment that frames a performance, keynote, or presentation: the structure, surfaces, lighting positions, and sightlines that determine what every person in the room sees and feels. A great stage doesn't just hold the show; it becomes part of the story.\n\nYou need stage design when a rental deck and a drape line won't carry the concept. A product launch that needs the reveal built into the architecture, a touring artist whose set piece has to fly in any orientation, a conference stage that has to read on camera as well as it reads in the room: these are design problems, not equipment problems.\n\nDone well, stage design disappears into the experience. The audience never thinks about load ratings or sightline studies. They just know the moment felt massive. That takes a team that designs, engineers, and builds under one roof."

For Create Our Future with Urban Outfitters x Adidas at Mack Sennett Studio in Los Angeles, the centerpiece was a projection-mapped 270-degree Cyclone Wall — a self-climbing truss system that wrapped the stage in full-surround visuals for live performances including Phantogram. For Destination with Journey's x Adidas at Horton Plaza Park in San Diego, we engineered a 40-foot tall structure that served as both stage presence and interactive canvas. And for Adriatique's worldwide X Future tour, Projekt X delivered a modular X-shaped set piece built from aircraft-grade aluminum with edge-lit DMX addressable LEDs — engineered to California safety standards, pixel-mappable, laser-mappable, and designed to fly in any orientation at any venue on the tour."

[services] services/projection-mapping (id 45)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE), _status=draft (admin-only draft state)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Projection mapping is the craft of turning real-world geometry into a display — a craft our Portland, Oregon team has practiced on canvases nationwide, from brand activations to the Empire State Building. Instead of projecting onto a flat screen, content is engineered to the exact contours of a building, a stage set, a sphere, or an entire room — so the surface itself appears to move, transform, and tell the story. Done right, the projector vanishes and the architecture comes alive.\n\nYou need projection mapping when the venue is the message. A product launch that transforms the room around the guests, a stage design that shifts scene without moving a single flat, a landmark facade that becomes election-night television — these are moments no LED wall can replicate, because the canvas already means something.\n\nThe discipline is equal parts art and metrology. Surfaces are measured, projectors are positioned and blended to the millimeter, and content is warped to real geometry. Beatrox handles that full chain — and has done it on canvases from a Manhattan bodega to the Empire State Building."
  AFTER:  "Projection mapping is the craft of turning real-world geometry into a display: a craft our Portland, Oregon team has practiced on canvases nationwide, from brand activations to the Empire State Building. Instead of projecting onto a flat screen, content is engineered to the exact contours of a building, a stage set, a sphere, or an entire room, so the surface itself appears to move, transform, and tell the story. Done right, the projector vanishes and the architecture comes alive.\n\nYou need projection mapping when the venue is the message. A product launch that transforms the room around the guests, a stage design that shifts scene without moving a single flat, a landmark facade that becomes election-night television: these are moments no LED wall can replicate, because the canvas already means something.\n\nThe discipline is equal parts art and metrology. Surfaces are measured, projectors are positioned and blended to the millimeter, and content is warped to real geometry. Beatrox handles that full chain, and has done it on canvases from a Manhattan bodega to the Empire State Building."

[services] services/projection-mapping (id 45)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE), _status=draft (admin-only draft state)
  field:  body[2].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Beatrox delivers projection mapping end to end: surface analysis, projector system design, content production, multi-projector blending and calibration, media server programming, and live operation. The same team that maps the surface produces the content — so what you approve in pre-visualization is what appears on the wall.\n\nFor the DUBAI 360 spherical projection theatre in Dubai Mall, we delivered content and deployment for DUBAI 360 & Obscura Digital on a fully spherical canvas — imagery engineered to surround the audience with no visible seams. With PROJECTING CHANGE: Racing Extinction for the Oceanic Preservation Society & Obscura Digital, projection content was designed for some of the world's most recognizable canvases, including the Empire State Building, the United Nations in New York, and the Vatican in Rome. For CNN: Road to 270 with CNN & Obscura Digital, we built projection and interactive visual content on the Empire State Building for election-night storytelling at landmark scale. And at Super Bowl 2020 in Miami, we transformed Ampersand Studios into an immersive Adidas VIP headquarters — custom projection content pre-visualized and calibrated to wrap the architecture of the venue in dynamic branded visuals for the full Super Bowl weekend."
  AFTER:  "Beatrox delivers projection mapping end to end: surface analysis, projector system design, content production, multi-projector blending and calibration, media server programming, and live operation. With PROJECTING CHANGE: Racing Extinction for the Oceanic Preservation Society & Obscura Digital, projection content was designed for some of the world's most recognizable canvases, including the Empire State Building, the United Nations in New York, and the Vatican in Rome. And at Super Bowl 2020 in Miami, we transformed Ampersand Studios into an immersive Adidas VIP headquarters: custom projection content pre-visualized and calibrated to wrap the architecture of the venue in dynamic branded visuals for the full Super Bowl weekend."

  BEFORE: "Multimedia displays are the screens, surfaces, and interactive devices that carry your content to an audience — LED video walls, kiosk systems, holographic and portal displays, ultra wide-format installations, and sensor-driven screens — designed and integrated by our Portland, Oregon team for events and venues nationwide. The hardware is only half the story; the integration, content pipeline, and reliability engineering are what make a display an experience instead of a TV.\n\nYou need multimedia display design whenever content has to live in the physical world: a festival booth that draws a crowd, a headquarters lobby that tells the company story, a retail environment where the display invites touch — or deliberately doesn't need it. Each context demands different brightness, pitch, interaction, and uptime.\n\nBeatrox approaches displays as systems, not rentals. We match panel and processor to the viewing distance, build the media server pipeline to the content, engineer the installation for its duty cycle, and stay accountable from power-up to strike — or for the life of a permanent install."

[services] services/immersive-environments (id 43)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE), _status=draft (admin-only draft state)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "An immersive environment is a space that stops behaving like a room — and Beatrox designs and builds them from Portland, Oregon for clients nationwide. Walls become forest canopy or deep ocean. Sound comes from everywhere. The floor, ceiling, and air itself are part of the design. Guests don't observe the experience — they're inside it, and their presence often drives what happens next.\n\nYou need an immersive environment when the message has to be felt, not read. Launching an outdoor jacket? Put guests in the forest. Raising ocean awareness? Sink them to the sea floor. The medium is the argument — and it only works when every technical system disappears behind the illusion.\n\nThat disappearance is the hard part. Projectors must hide inside the scenery, sensors must track without a single visible wire, and content must wrap real geometry without a seam. Beatrox builds environments where the technology is invisible and the world is all that's left."
  AFTER:  "An immersive environment is a space that stops behaving like a room, and Beatrox designs and builds them from Portland, Oregon for clients nationwide. Walls become forest canopy or deep ocean. Sound comes from everywhere. The floor, ceiling, and air itself are part of the design. Guests don't observe the experience. They're inside it, and their presence often drives what happens next.\n\nYou need an immersive environment when the message has to be felt, not read. Launching an outdoor jacket? Put guests in the forest. Raising ocean awareness? Sink them to the sea floor. The medium is the argument, and it only works when every technical system disappears behind the illusion.\n\nThat disappearance is the hard part. Projectors must hide inside the scenery, sensors must track without a single visible wire, and content must wrap real geometry without a seam. Beatrox builds environments where the technology is invisible and the world is all that's left."

[services] services/audio-production (id 41)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE), _status=draft (admin-only draft state)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Audio production is the engineering discipline behind what an audience hears — system design, tuning, mixing, and the hundred real-time decisions that keep a show sounding effortless — and our Portland, Oregon audio team delivers it for concerts and events nationwide. It's distinct from equipment rental: the same PA can sound world-class or amateur depending on who designs the coverage, rings out the room, and stands at the console.\n\nYou need dedicated audio production whenever the show has stakes. A festival set where the artist's mix has to translate to a field of ten thousand, a keynote where the CEO's words have to reach the back row without a hint of feedback, a livestream where the broadcast mix is the only one that matters. Each demands engineers who've done it under pressure.\n\nBeatrox staffs audio the way tours do: an A1 at front of house owning the room mix, an A2 managing monitors and wireless, systems tuned before doors, and RF coordinated so nothing drops mid-chorus. The audience never thinks about audio — which is exactly the point."
  AFTER:  "Audio production is the engineering discipline behind what an audience hears (system design, tuning, mixing, and the hundred real-time decisions that keep a show sounding effortless) and our Portland, Oregon audio team delivers it for concerts and events nationwide. It's distinct from equipment rental: the same PA can sound world-class or amateur depending on who designs the coverage, rings out the room, and stands at the console.\n\nYou need dedicated audio production whenever the show has stakes. A festival set where the artist's mix has to translate to a field of ten thousand, a keynote where the CEO's words have to reach the back row without a hint of feedback, a livestream where the broadcast mix is the only one that matters. Each demands engineers who've done it under pressure.\n\nBeatrox staffs audio the way tours do: an A1 at front of house owning the room mix, an A2 managing monitors and wireless, systems tuned before doors, and RF coordinated so nothing drops mid-chorus. The audience never thinks about audio, which is exactly the point."

[services] services/venue-sourcing-booking (id 40)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Venue sourcing is the search for a physical space that can actually hold your event — creatively and technically — a search Beatrox runs from Portland, Oregon for productions nationwide. It's easy to find a room; it's harder to find one with the footprint, ceiling height, power, load-in access, and neighborhood character your experience demands, at terms your budget can survive. Booking is the second half: negotiating the contract, deposits, insurance requirements, and house rules that govern what you're allowed to build.\n\nFor experiential work, this step carries extra weight. Immersive environments, projection mapping, and interactive installations impose requirements a standard ballroom rarely meets — which is why the most memorable activations often happen in raw warehouses, shuttered retail, rooftops, and public parks. Those spaces deliver unmatched creative freedom, but they also demand a producer who can assess feasibility and negotiate with landlords and municipalities who don't deal with events every day.\n\nYou need venue sourcing support whenever the space itself is part of the concept — or whenever you simply can't afford to discover a venue's deal-breakers after the deposit clears."
  AFTER:  "Venue sourcing is the search for a physical space that can actually hold your event, creatively and technically. Beatrox runs that search from Portland, Oregon for productions nationwide. It's easy to find a room; it's harder to find one with the footprint, ceiling height, power, load-in access, and neighborhood character your experience demands, at terms your budget can survive. Booking is the second half: negotiating the contract, deposits, insurance requirements, and house rules that govern what you're allowed to build.\n\nFor experiential work, this step carries extra weight. Immersive environments, projection mapping, and interactive installations impose requirements a standard ballroom rarely meets, which is why the most memorable activations often happen in raw warehouses, shuttered retail, rooftops, and public parks. Those spaces deliver unmatched creative freedom, but they also demand a producer who can assess feasibility and negotiate with landlords and municipalities who don't deal with events every day.\n\nYou need venue sourcing support whenever the space itself is part of the concept, or whenever you simply can't afford to discover a venue's deal-breakers after the deposit clears."

[services] services/venue-sourcing-booking (id 40)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[2].content

Because we're builders, our feasibility reads are practical: we know what it costs to make a raw space show-ready, so there are no surprises in the budget.\n\nThis approach shaped projects like MyShelter, where we converted a shut-down, narrow Manhattan bodega — previously used for storing old kitchen equipment — into a fully immersive forest environment for the Adidas Outdoor jacket launch, hiding all technical infrastructure within a tiny footprint. [services] services/trade-convention-booths (id 39)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "A trade or convention booth is a brand's entire world compressed into a few hundred square feet — and Beatrox designs, fabricates, and installs them from Portland, Oregon for exhibitors nationwide. [services] services/tour-management (id 38)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Tour management is the production discipline behind anything that happens more than once, in more than one place — a discipline Beatrox runs from Portland, Oregon for programs routing across the country. Whether it's a brand activation hitting multiple festivals, a product experience moving city to city, or a live show with a routing sheet, the challenge is the same: deliver an identical quality of experience in venues that are never identical.\n\nEach stop brings new constraints — different footprints, power, load-in access, local vendors, and timelines. Without a touring framework, every city becomes a ground-up rebuild: budgets drift, freight goes missing, and the show slowly mutates as teams improvise around surprises. Tour management replaces improvisation with a system — advance work, spec packs, repeatable builds, and documentation that travels with the production.\n\nYou need it whenever your program has more than one date on the calendar. The second stop is where single-event thinking breaks down, and it's where an experienced tour manager pays for themselves immediately."
  AFTER:  "Tour management is the production discipline behind anything that happens more than once, in more than one place: a discipline Beatrox runs from Portland, Oregon for programs routing across the country. Whether it's a brand activation hitting multiple festivals, a product experience moving city to city, or a live show with a routing sheet, the challenge is the same: deliver an identical quality of experience in venues that are never identical.\n\nEach stop brings new constraints: different footprints, power, load-in access, local vendors, and timelines. Without a touring framework, every city becomes a ground-up rebuild: budgets drift, freight goes missing, and the show slowly mutates as teams improvise around surprises. Tour management replaces improvisation with a system: advance work, spec packs, repeatable builds, and documentation that travels with the production.\n\nYou need it whenever your program has more than one date on the calendar. The second stop is where single-event thinking breaks down, and it's where an experienced tour manager pays for themselves immediately."

[services] services/technical-documentation (id 37)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Technical documentation is the written record of how a production or installation actually works — documentation Beatrox produces from Portland, Oregon for events and installations nationwide: system diagrams, signal flow, equipment schedules, operating procedures, maintenance guides, and as-built records. It's what allows someone who didn't build the system to run it, fix it, or rebuild it.\n\nYou need technical documentation whenever a system outlives the crew that installed it — a touring show with rotating technicians, a permanent installation maintained by in-house staff, or a brand activation that will be rebuilt in the next city. Without documentation, every handover is a game of telephone and every fault becomes a forensic investigation.\n\nDocumentation also protects the production itself. When the show manual defines power-up sequences, fallback procedures, and who owns which system, show day stops depending on any single person's memory. That's not bureaucracy — that's reliability."
  AFTER:  "Technical documentation is the written record of how a production or installation actually works: documentation Beatrox produces from Portland, Oregon for events and installations nationwide: system diagrams, signal flow, equipment schedules, operating procedures, maintenance guides, and as-built records. [services] services/technical-direction (id 36)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Technical direction is the discipline of turning a creative concept into a working production — a discipline Beatrox provides from Portland, Oregon for events and experiential builds nationwide. A technical director (TD) sits between the design team and the build crew, owning every technical system on a project — audio, video, lighting, projection, rigging, power, networking, and interactive technology — and making sure they all work together, on budget, on schedule, and safely.\n\nYou need technical direction whenever a project has more moving parts than one person can track: multiple vendors, custom-fabricated elements, complex signal flow, or a venue with real constraints. The TD reads the drawings, questions the assumptions, coordinates the departments, and makes the hundreds of small decisions that determine whether load-in takes eight hours or eighteen.\n\nGood technical direction is invisible when it works. The show opens on time, the systems behave, and the creative team never has to think about the infrastructure underneath their vision. That invisibility is earned in pre-production — long before the first truck arrives."
  AFTER:  "Technical direction is the discipline of turning a creative concept into a working production: a discipline Beatrox provides from Portland, Oregon for events and experiential builds nationwide. A technical director (TD) sits between the design team and the build crew, owning every technical system on a project (audio, video, lighting, projection, rigging, power, networking, and interactive technology) and making sure they all work together, on budget, on schedule, and safely.\n\nYou need technical direction whenever a project has more moving parts than one person can track: multiple vendors, custom-fabricated elements, complex signal flow, or a venue with real constraints. The TD reads the drawings, questions the assumptions, coordinates the departments, and makes the hundreds of small decisions that determine whether load-in takes eight hours or eighteen.\n\nGood technical direction is invisible when it works. The show opens on time, the systems behave, and the creative team never has to think about the infrastructure underneath their vision. That invisibility is earned in pre-production, long before the first truck arrives."

[services] services/software-development (id 32)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Event software development is the craft of building custom code that has to work perfectly, in public, at a specific moment in time — a craft Beatrox practices from Portland, Oregon for experiences nationwide. It covers real-time interactive experiences, sensor-driven installations, media playback logic, show-control integration, and the custom tooling that ties lighting, video, audio, and interactivity into one responsive system.\n\nYou need custom software whenever an experience can't be bought off the shelf: a wall that reacts to the people in front of it, an AR moment driven by body tracking, a queue of kiosks that must never freeze, or a show where lighting and projection respond live to a performance. The tools exist — TouchDesigner, Unreal Engine, Unity, computer vision libraries — but making them reliable under live conditions is its own discipline.\n\nWhat separates event software from other software is the testing environment: there isn't one. Code has to be right before doors open, degrade gracefully if a sensor misbehaves, and be operable by a crew member who isn't a developer. That's how we build it."
  AFTER:  "Event software development is the craft of building custom code that has to work perfectly, in public, at a specific moment in time: a craft Beatrox practices from Portland, Oregon for experiences nationwide. It covers real-time interactive experiences, sensor-driven installations, media playback logic, show-control integration, and the custom tooling that ties lighting, video, audio, and interactivity into one responsive system.\n\nYou need custom software whenever an experience can't be bought off the shelf: a wall that reacts to the people in front of it, an AR moment driven by body tracking, a queue of kiosks that must never freeze, or a show where lighting and projection respond live to a performance. The tools exist (TouchDesigner, Unreal Engine, Unity, computer vision libraries), but making them reliable under live conditions is its own discipline.\n\nWhat separates event software from other software is the testing environment: there isn't one. Code has to be right before doors open, degrade gracefully if a sensor misbehaves, and be operable by a crew member who isn't a developer. That's how we build it."

[services] services/site-floor-plans (id 31)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Site and floor plans are the scaled drawings that define where everything goes — drawings our Portland, Oregon team produces for events and experiential builds nationwide: stages, screens, scenic, seating, bars, back-of-house, cable runs, power drops, and the paths guests and crew will take through the space. They're the single document every department — production, AV, scenic, catering, security, the venue itself — works from.\n\nYou need proper site and floor plans whenever an event occupies real space, which is always. A venue's marketing PDF is rarely dimensioned, never shows column locations accurately, and says nothing about power or rigging points. Building your layout on accurate surveyed measurements is what separates a smooth load-in from a day of shuffling trucks and redrawing on a whiteboard.\n\nGood plans also make the invisible visible: guest flow bottlenecks, sightline obstructions, cable paths that cross emergency egress, a projector throw that clips a chandelier. Finding those problems on paper costs nothing. Finding them on site costs a day."
  AFTER:  "Site and floor plans are the scaled drawings that define where everything goes: drawings our Portland, Oregon team produces for events and experiential builds nationwide: stages, screens, scenic, seating, bars, back-of-house, cable runs, power drops, and the paths guests and crew will take through the space. They're the single document every department (production, AV, scenic, catering, security, the venue itself) works from.\n\nYou need proper site and floor plans whenever an event occupies real space, which is always. A venue's marketing PDF is rarely dimensioned, never shows column locations accurately, and says nothing about power or rigging points. Building your layout on accurate surveyed measurements is what separates a smooth load-in from a day of shuffling trucks and redrawing on a whiteboard.\n\nGood plans also make the invisible visible: guest flow bottlenecks, sightline obstructions, cable paths that cross emergency egress, a projector throw that clips a chandelier. Load-ins are short, venues are unforgiving, and every piece has to land in the right order. A seasoned assembly crew makes a complex environment come together in hours instead of days."

[services] services/production-management (id 28)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Production management is the operational leadership of an event or build — the role that owns the budget, the schedule, and the truth about where the project actually stands — and Beatrox provides it from Portland, Oregon for complex experiential events nationwide. While creative teams shape the experience and technical teams build it, the production manager makes sure every department has what it needs, when it needs it, at a cost the project can bear.\n\nComplex experiential work fails in the gaps between departments: the fabricator waiting on a sign-off, the AV vendor quoting against an outdated drawing, the venue approving a schedule nobody circulated. A production manager closes those gaps — running the master schedule, tracking every dollar, coordinating vendors, and giving the client a single honest answer to 'where are we?'\n\nYou need dedicated production management whenever multiple departments, partners, or workstreams must land together — which describes nearly every ambitious event. It's the difference between a project that's merely busy and one that's actually under control."
  AFTER:  "Production management is the operational leadership of an event or build (the role that owns the budget, the schedule, and the truth about where the project actually stands) and Beatrox provides it from Portland, Oregon for complex experiential events nationwide. [services] services/pre-visualization (id 27)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Pre-visualization — pre-viz — is the practice of building your event in 3D before it exists in reality, a service our Portland, Oregon team provides for productions nationwide. Using accurate models of the venue, staging, lighting, and projection surfaces, we render exactly what the audience will see, from any seat, at any moment of the show.\n\nYou need pre-viz whenever the stakes are too high for guesswork. Large-format projection mapping, complex lighting rigs, multi-screen stages, and one-night-only brand moments all benefit enormously from seeing — and approving — the final result in advance. Pre-viz turns subjective debates into clear decisions.\n\nIt's also a powerful risk-reduction tool. Sightline problems, projector alignment issues, and lighting conflicts are all cheap to fix in a 3D model and expensive to fix on site. Pre-visualization pays for itself by eliminating surprises."
  AFTER:  "Pre-visualization (pre-viz) is the practice of building your event in 3D before it exists in reality, a service our Portland, Oregon team provides for productions nationwide. Using accurate models of the venue, staging, lighting, and projection surfaces, we render exactly what the audience will see, from any seat, at any moment of the show.\n\nYou need pre-viz whenever the stakes are too high for guesswork. Large-format projection mapping, complex lighting rigs, multi-screen stages, and one-night-only brand moments all benefit enormously from seeing (and approving) the final result in advance. Pre-viz turns subjective debates into clear decisions.\n\nIt's also a powerful risk-reduction tool. Sightline problems, projector alignment issues, and lighting conflicts are all cheap to fix in a 3D model and expensive to fix on site. Pre-visualization pays for itself by eliminating surprises."

[services] services/pre-visualization (id 27)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[2].content

[services] services/permit-submittal (id 26)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Permit submittal is the process of getting official approval for everything your event does that a city, venue, or agency regulates — a process Beatrox manages from Portland, Oregon for events nationwide: amplified sound, temporary structures, rigging over audiences, open flame and effects, occupancy changes, street or park use, and specialized systems like show lasers. Each approval lives with a different authority, runs on its own timeline, and demands its own documentation.\n\nIt's the least glamorous line in the budget and one of the most dangerous to ignore. A missed permit can shut down load-in, force a last-minute redesign, or cancel an activation outright — and agencies remember productions that try to skip the process. Submittals also aren't just forms: they require stamped drawings, engineering calculations, safety plans, and someone who can answer an inspector's technical questions in their language.\n\nYou need dedicated permit management whenever your event touches public space, builds temporary structures, runs effects, or operates anything regulators classify as special — which, in experiential production, is most of the interesting work."
  AFTER:  "Permit submittal is the process of getting official approval for everything your event does that a city, venue, or agency regulates: a process Beatrox manages from Portland, Oregon for events nationwide: amplified sound, temporary structures, rigging over audiences, open flame and effects, occupancy changes, street or park use, and specialized systems like show lasers. Each approval lives with a different authority, runs on its own timeline, and demands its own documentation.\n\nIt's the least glamorous line in the budget and one of the most dangerous to ignore. A missed permit can shut down load-in, force a last-minute redesign, or cancel an activation outright, and agencies remember productions that try to skip the process. Submittals also aren't just forms: they require stamped drawings, engineering calculations, safety plans, and someone who can answer an inspector's technical questions in their language.\n\nYou need dedicated permit management whenever your event touches public space, builds temporary structures, runs effects, or operates anything regulators classify as special, which, in experiential production, is most of the interesting work."

[services] services/media-server-playback-solutions (id 24)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Media servers are the engines that store, process, and play back video content across complex display systems — systems Beatrox designs, programs, and operates from Portland, Oregon for shows nationwide — warping and blending multiple projectors onto irregular surfaces, driving LED walls at native pixel maps, and keeping every screen in a venue synchronized to the frame. Playback solutions cover the whole chain: server hardware, software programming, content formatting, signal distribution, and live operation.\n\nYou need a media server specialist whenever content has to land precisely on a surface that isn't a flat rectangle, whenever more than one display must move as one image, or whenever playback simply cannot fail in front of an audience. Mapping a building, wrapping a dome, or syncing a dozen screens to a musical performance is a playback engineering problem before it's a creative one.\n\nThe difference between a good show and a flawless one is usually invisible: redundant servers, frame-accurate timecode, content tested at show resolution before load-in. That preparation is the service."
  AFTER:  "Media servers are the engines that store, process, and play back video content across complex display systems (systems Beatrox designs, programs, and operates from Portland, Oregon for shows nationwide), warping and blending multiple projectors onto irregular surfaces, driving LED walls at native pixel maps, and keeping every screen in a venue synchronized to the frame. Playback solutions cover the whole chain: server hardware, software programming, content formatting, signal distribution, and live operation.\n\nYou need a media server specialist whenever content has to land precisely on a surface that isn't a flat rectangle, whenever more than one display must move as one image, or whenever playback simply cannot fail in front of an audience. [services] services/lighting-integration (id 21)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Lighting integration is the discipline of making every light-emitting element in a space — LED video panels, pixel tape, theatrical fixtures, interactive sensors — behave as one coordinated system, a discipline Beatrox delivers from Portland, Oregon on events and installations nationwide. It's not just hanging fixtures; it's designing the power, data, control, and programming layers that let all of it respond together.\n\nYou need lighting integration whenever a space has to do more than be lit: when visuals must sync to live music, when a wall should react to a person's movement, or when an environment needs to shift mood on cue. Modern experiential work lives at this intersection — LED video, interactive media, and architectural light are no longer separate trades.\n\nPoorly integrated lighting is obvious: flicker on camera, mismatched color, cues that drift. Well-integrated lighting disappears into the experience — audiences just know the room felt alive. That outcome is engineered, not accidental."
  AFTER:  "Lighting integration is the discipline of making every light-emitting element in a space (LED video panels, pixel tape, theatrical fixtures, interactive sensors) behave as one coordinated system, a discipline Beatrox delivers from Portland, Oregon on events and installations nationwide. [services] services/lighting-integration (id 21)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[2].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Beatrox integrates lighting as part of the whole system: LED video panel specification and installation, addressable LED and pixel control, sensor-driven interactivity, show programming, and the power and data infrastructure that ties it together. Our programmers work in TouchDesigner and real-time engines, so lighting, video content, and interactive logic are designed in the same pipeline and calibrated together on site.\n\nFor Run for the Oceans at Domino Park in Brooklyn, created for Adidas and Parley Ocean School, we fitted a custom shipping container build with seamless wall-to-wall ROE Black Pearl LED video panels and mirror surfaces to create an infinity-mirror light environment — with scanning LIDAR sensors driving real-time flocking and fluid simulations that responded to every participant's movement. For Destination in San Diego, built for Journey's x Adidas, we integrated the control layer behind a 40-foot interactive canvas: Arduino firmware, relay-driven mechanical valves, and TouchDesigner synchronization that translated a guest's button press into paint flowing down the structure in real time. And for The Great Escape, an Adidas product launch experience in Los Angeles, we delivered integrated creative technology and production workflows — the lighting, media, and show systems working as one."
  AFTER:  "Beatrox integrates lighting as part of the whole system: LED video panel specification and installation, addressable LED and pixel control, sensor-driven interactivity, show programming, and the power and data infrastructure that ties it together.   BEFORE: "Labor hire is how productions get the specialist crew they need without carrying a full-time staff — and Beatrox supplies vetted event crew from Portland, Oregon to shows nationwide. Live events run on defined roles — the TD owns the technical systems, the PM owns schedule and budget, the A1 mixes front of house while the A2 runs monitors and RF, the V1 drives the screens while the V2 manages playback and switching, and the L1 designs and calls the lighting with the L2 on programming and focus. Each role is a career skill, and shows succeed or fail on who's sitting in those chairs.\n\nThe challenge for most producers is finding people who are genuinely good — not just available. A resume full of acronyms doesn't tell you how someone behaves at hour fourteen of a problem load-in, or whether they can hold a show together when a playback system hiccups mid-keynote.\n\nThat's what a labor partner solves: vetted professionals, matched to the actual demands of your show, booked under one accountable umbrella. You get the right number of the right people — no more, no less — plus the supervision and insurance structure that comes with an established production company."

[services] services/interactive-ui-ux-design (id 16)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Interactive UI/UX design for events is a different discipline from app or web design — one our Portland, Oregon team practices on kiosks, installations, and brand experiences nationwide. Your 'user' is a guest walking through a loud, crowded space with seconds of attention to spare — and the interface might be a touchscreen kiosk, a motion-tracked wall, or a display that reacts to people simply passing by. Design has to work at walking speed, with zero instructions.\n\nYou need this service whenever people will touch, move, or gesture to control content: interactive kiosks, AR mirrors, timeline walls, product explorers, or gamified brand moments. The stakes are high — a confusing interface doesn't just frustrate one user, it creates a visible crowd of confused guests in the middle of your event.\n\nGreat experiential UX is invisible. Guests should never wonder what to do next; the interface should invite, respond, and reward in a loop that feels natural the first time — because at a live event, the first time is usually the only time."
  AFTER:  "Interactive UI/UX design for events is a different discipline from app or web design: one our Portland, Oregon team practices on kiosks, installations, and brand experiences nationwide. Your 'user' is a guest walking through a loud, crowded space with seconds of attention to spare. The interface might be a touchscreen kiosk, a motion-tracked wall, or a display that reacts to people simply passing by. Design has to work at walking speed, with zero instructions.\n\nYou need this service whenever people will touch, move, or gesture to control content: interactive kiosks, AR mirrors, timeline walls, product explorers, or gamified brand moments. The stakes are high. A confusing interface doesn't just frustrate one user, it creates a visible crowd of confused guests in the middle of your event.\n\nGreat experiential UX is invisible. Guests should never wonder what to do next; the interface should invite, respond, and reward in a loop that feels natural the first time, because at a live event, the first time is usually the only time."

[services] services/interactive-ui-ux-design (id 16)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[2].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "We design the complete interaction layer: UX flows mapped to real guest journeys, interface layouts prototyped in Figma, on-brand visual systems, and the motion, sound, and feedback design that makes interactions feel responsive. Critically, we design for throughput — session lengths, attract loops, and queue behavior — and we test with real people before deployment. Our designers work alongside the engineers building the system, so the UX accounts for sensor accuracy, latency, and hardware limits from the start.\n\nAt AKU World for AKU + FTX in New York, guests used interactive kiosks to choose their 'Inner Astronaut' and flow through avatar creation — interface design that had to be intuitive enough for a continuous stream of event attendees. For Amazon Music Live's Infinite Playlist Tour at Outside Lands and Stagecoach 2023, we designed interactive touchpoints throughout the festival builds — AR moments, photo moments, and giveaway flows built for high-volume foot traffic. And for the G-MAN Experiential Campaign with Toyota x MTV in Los Angeles, we applied the same guest-first interface thinking to a national experiential campaign."
  AFTER:  "We design the complete interaction layer: UX flows mapped to real guest journeys, interface layouts prototyped in Figma, on-brand visual systems, and the motion, sound, and feedback design that makes interactions feel responsive. Critically, we design for throughput (session lengths, attract loops, and queue behavior) and we test with real people before deployment. Our designers work alongside the engineers building the system, so the UX accounts for sensor accuracy, latency, and hardware limits from the start.\n\nAt AKU World for AKU + FTX in New York, guests used interactive kiosks to choose their 'Inner Astronaut' and flow through avatar creation: interface design that had to be intuitive enough for a continuous stream of event attendees. [services] services/event-planning-logistics (id 14)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Event planning and logistics is the discipline that turns a creative concept into a functioning, on-schedule reality — a discipline Beatrox runs from Portland, Oregon for events across the country. It covers everything between the approved design and the last truck pulling away from the venue: production timelines, vendor contracts, budgets, freight, crew schedules, venue coordination, and the hundreds of small decisions that determine whether doors open on time.\n\nYou need this layer of support whenever an event has more moving parts than one person can hold in their head — which, in experiential production, is almost always. Multi-room builds, live performances, interactive installations, and public-site activations each bring their own vendors, approvals, and dependencies, and they all have to land in the same window.\n\nDone well, logistics is invisible: guests experience a seamless event while a tightly managed machine runs behind it. Done poorly, it's the reason load-in runs four hours late. We treat planning as a creative enabler — the structure that lets ambitious ideas actually happen."
  AFTER:  "Event planning and logistics is the discipline that turns a creative concept into a functioning, on-schedule reality: a discipline Beatrox runs from Portland, Oregon for events across the country. It covers everything between the approved design and the last truck pulling away from the venue: production timelines, vendor contracts, budgets, freight, crew schedules, venue coordination, and the hundreds of small decisions that determine whether doors open on time.\n\nYou need this layer of support whenever an event has more moving parts than one person can hold in their head, which, in experiential production, is almost always. Multi-room builds, live performances, interactive installations, and public-site activations each bring their own vendors, approvals, and dependencies, and they all have to land in the same window.\n\nDone well, logistics is invisible: guests experience a seamless event while a tightly managed machine runs behind it. Done poorly, it's the reason load-in runs four hours late. We treat planning as a creative enabler: the structure that lets ambitious ideas actually happen."

[services] services/environmental-design (id 13)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Environmental design is the discipline of shaping a physical space into a coherent, branded place — work our Portland, Oregon team delivers for festivals, exhibitions, and brand environments nationwide. It covers the spatial plan — how guests enter, where they gather, what they see first — and the physical layer on top of it: scenic elements, materials, finishes, graphics, and signage. Where immersive environments lean on media and technology to transport guests, environmental design makes the space itself do the work.\n\nYou need environmental design whenever a venue has to function as more than a container. Festival footprints, exhibition builds, pop-up retail, and brand pavilions all live or die on layout, sightlines, and finish quality — and on whether the design can actually be built, permitted, and struck within the real constraints of the site.\n\nThat's where our version of the discipline earns its keep: Beatrox designs environments with fabrication and production in the same room. Every concept accounts for rigging, power, load paths, and guest flow from day one, so the design you approve is the environment that gets built."
  AFTER:  "Environmental design is the discipline of shaping a physical space into a coherent, branded place: work our Portland, Oregon team delivers for festivals, exhibitions, and brand environments nationwide. It covers the spatial plan (how guests enter, where they gather, what they see first) and the physical layer on top of it: scenic elements, materials, finishes, graphics, and signage. Where immersive environments lean on media and technology to transport guests, environmental design makes the space itself do the work.\n\nYou need environmental design whenever a venue has to function as more than a container. Festival footprints, exhibition builds, pop-up retail, and brand pavilions all live or die on layout, sightlines, and finish quality, and on whether the design can actually be built, permitted, and struck within the real constraints of the site.\n\nThat's where our version of the discipline earns its keep: Beatrox designs environments with fabrication and production in the same room. Every concept accounts for rigging, power, load paths, and guest flow from day one, so the design you approve is the environment that gets built."

[services] services/engineering-certification (id 12)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  hero.subheadline
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Structural analysis and stamped engineering documentation that gets ambitious builds approved — and keeps them safe in the real world."
  AFTER:  "Structural analysis and stamped engineering documentation that gets ambitious builds approved, and keeps them safe in the real world."

[services] services/engineering-certification (id 12)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Engineering certification is the formal verification that a structure is safe to build, suspend, and stand in front of an audience — a service Beatrox coordinates from Portland, Oregon for event structures nationwide. A licensed engineer reviews the design — its materials, connections, loads, and the forces it will see from rigging, crowds, weather, and its own weight — and stamps drawings that venues, fire marshals, and insurers require before they'll let anything go up.\n\nYou need engineering certification whenever a build goes beyond off-the-shelf: custom-fabricated scenic, flown elements, tall freestanding structures, outdoor festival builds, or anything a venue's operations team asks questions about. Many venues and municipalities won't approve a load-in without stamped documents, and discovering that late is one of the most expensive surprises in production.\n\nCertification isn't a hurdle to clear — it's design input. When engineering thinking starts early, structures get lighter, rigging gets simpler, and approvals get faster."
  AFTER:  "Engineering certification is the formal verification that a structure is safe to build, suspend, and stand in front of an audience: a service Beatrox coordinates from Portland, Oregon for event structures nationwide. A licensed engineer reviews the design (its materials, connections, loads, and the forces it will see from rigging, crowds, weather, and its own weight) and stamps drawings that venues, fire marshals, and insurers require before they'll let anything go up.\n\nYou need engineering certification whenever a build goes beyond off-the-shelf: custom-fabricated scenic, flown elements, tall freestanding structures, outdoor festival builds, or anything a venue's operations team asks questions about. Many venues and municipalities won't approve a load-in without stamped documents, and discovering that late is one of the most expensive surprises in production.\n\nCertification isn't a hurdle to clear: it's design input. When engineering thinking starts early, structures get lighter, rigging gets simpler, and approvals get faster."

[services] services/engineering-certification (id 12)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[2].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "BEATROX manages the full certification path: we prepare the calculation packages and drawing sets, coordinate with licensed engineers, and deliver stamped documents in the format venues and authorities expect. Because our drafters, fabricators, and technical directors sit in the same shop, the certified design is the same design that gets built — no translation losses between engineering intent and shop reality.\n\nWe've engineered structures for demanding environments. On AKU World for AKU + FTX in New York, the build included a custom-fabricated large-scale helmet installation alongside projection-mapped rooms and holographic displays — structural elements that had to be documented and safe for a public event. The Infinite Playlist Tour for Amazon Music Live deployed custom builds at Outside Lands and Stagecoach, where outdoor festival structures face wind and weather loads that must be calculated, not guessed. And at Super Bowl 2020, we transformed Ampersand Studios in Miami into an immersive Adidas VIP headquarters, where every suspended and freestanding element in a guest-filled venue needed to be engineered and approved."
  AFTER:  "BEATROX manages the full certification path: we prepare the calculation packages and drawing sets, coordinate with licensed engineers, and deliver stamped documents in the format venues and authorities expect. Because our drafters, fabricators, and technical directors sit in the same shop, the certified design is the same design that gets built, no translation losses between engineering intent and shop reality.\n\nWe've engineered structures for demanding environments. On AKU World for AKU + FTX in New York, the build included a custom-fabricated large-scale helmet installation alongside projection-mapped rooms and holographic displays: structural elements that had to be documented and safe for a public event. And at Super Bowl 2020, we transformed Ampersand Studios in Miami into an immersive Adidas VIP headquarters, where every suspended and freestanding element in a guest-filled venue needed to be engineered and approved."

[services] services/drafting-detail-drawings (id 10)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Drafting is how an idea becomes a set of instructions — and from our Portland, Oregon studio, Beatrox produces the detail drawings that keep experiential builds across the country on the same page. In experiential production, detail drawings are the shared language between designers, fabricators, engineers, riggers, and venue staff — dimensioned plans, sections, elevations, and shop drawings that define exactly what gets built, where it goes, and how it fits together.\n\nYou need professional drafting whenever something has to be fabricated, approved, or coordinated across departments. A scenic wall that looks simple in a render can hide dozens of decisions: material thicknesses, connection details, cable paths, access panels, sightline clearances. Without drawings, those decisions get made on the shop floor or on site — the two most expensive places to make them.\n\nGood drafting also protects the creative. When every vendor works from the same accurate documents, the finished build matches the approved design, change orders shrink, and load-in stops being a negotiation."
  AFTER:  "Drafting is how an idea becomes a set of instructions, and from our Portland, Oregon studio, Beatrox produces the detail drawings that keep experiential builds across the country on the same page. In experiential production, detail drawings are the shared language between designers, fabricators, engineers, riggers, and venue staff: dimensioned plans, sections, elevations, and shop drawings that define exactly what gets built, where it goes, and how it fits together.\n\nYou need professional drafting whenever something has to be fabricated, approved, or coordinated across departments. A scenic wall that looks simple in a render can hide dozens of decisions: material thicknesses, connection details, cable paths, access panels, sightline clearances. Without drawings, those decisions get made on the shop floor or on site: the two most expensive places to make them.\n\nGood drafting also protects the creative. When every vendor works from the same accurate documents, the finished build matches the approved design, change orders shrink, and load-in stops being a negotiation."

[services] services/custom-fabrication (id 8)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE), _status=draft (admin-only draft state)
  field:  body[2].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Beatrox handles the full fabrication arc in-house: design development, engineering, shop drawings, prototyping, build, finishing, and on-site installation. Our shop works across wood, metal, and acrylic, and we routinely integrate electronics — custom controllers, firmware, relay systems, and mechanical actuation — directly into the pieces we build, so the physical and technical sides of a project are solved by the same team.\n\nFor Destination, created for Journey's x Adidas at Horton Plaza Park in San Diego, we designed and built a 40-foot tall structure skinned with a blank canvas and outfitted with a custom-built robotic paint distribution device. Participants selected one of five paint colors with a custom button controller, and our Arduino firmware drove a relay-based mechanical valve system that sent paint flowing down the canvas in real time — all synchronized with TouchDesigner. For FLIR, we delivered a permanent interactive history wall at FLIR's Washington, DC headquarters, integrating ultra wide-format displays, LIDAR sensors, and a media server into a single installation engineered for reliability and low maintenance. And for El Camino, we produced a Comic-Con exhibition build for Netflix in New York — a high-traffic environment where durability and finish quality had to hold up under constant public interaction."

[services] services/consultation-system-design (id 7)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "Consultation and system design is the thinking that happens before the spending — and Beatrox provides it from Portland, Oregon for events and installations nationwide. It's the process of translating a creative goal — an interactive wall, a flagship event, a permanent installation — into a concrete technical system: what equipment, in what configuration, at what cost, maintained by whom.\n\nYou need this service whenever the technical path isn't obvious. Maybe you're weighing LED against projection, temporary rental against permanent install, or wondering whether a bold creative idea is even feasible in your venue. An experienced systems consultant answers those questions with numbers and drawings, not guesses.\n\nGood system design saves money twice: once by speccing only what you actually need, and again by preventing the expensive mistakes — undersized power, incompatible formats, unmaintainable installations — that plague under-planned projects."
  AFTER:  "Consultation and system design is the thinking that happens before the spending, and Beatrox provides it from Portland, Oregon for events and installations nationwide. [services] services/cnc-machining (id 6)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "CNC machining uses computer-controlled cutting tools to turn digital designs into physical parts with millimeter precision — work our Portland, Oregon shop produces for experiential builds across the country. In experiential production, it's how crisp signage, intricate scenic details, interlocking structures, and perfectly repeatable components get made — straight from a CAD file to the shop floor.\n\nYou need CNC machining whenever a design calls for accuracy hand tools can't hold: tight-tolerance display housings, layered acrylic graphics, branded dimensional lettering, or dozens of identical parts that have to fit together on a fast load-in. It's also the fastest path from prototype to production — once the file is right, the hundredth part is identical to the first.\n\nThe real advantage isn't the machine; it's having machining in the same shop as fabrication, electronics, and finishing. Parts are designed for how they'll actually be assembled and used — not just how they look on screen."
  AFTER:  "CNC machining uses computer-controlled cutting tools to turn digital designs into physical parts with millimeter precision: work our Portland, Oregon shop produces for experiential builds across the country. [services] services/av-system-integration (id 4)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "AV system integration is the work of making separate technologies behave as one system — work our Portland, Oregon team delivers on events and installations nationwide. Audio, video, lighting, interactivity, and control each arrive from different vendors with different protocols — integration is the design, infrastructure, and programming that binds them together so a single cue can move every department at once.\n\nYou need integration whenever a project combines more than one technical discipline — which is nearly every experiential build. A projection-mapped room with spatial audio and reactive lighting isn't three systems; it's one experience. Without deliberate integration, you get three teams, three timelines, and a show where the pieces never quite land together.\n\nIntegration done well is mostly invisible infrastructure: clean signal flow, sensible network architecture, labeled racks, and control logic that lets an operator run a complex show from one surface. It's the difference between technology that supports the creative and technology that competes with it."
  AFTER:  "AV system integration is the work of making separate technologies behave as one system: work our Portland, Oregon team delivers on events and installations nationwide. Audio, video, lighting, interactivity, and control each arrive from different vendors with different protocols. Integration is the design, infrastructure, and programming that binds them together so a single cue can move every department at once.\n\nYou need integration whenever a project combines more than one technical discipline, which is nearly every experiential build. A projection-mapped room with spatial audio and reactive lighting isn't three systems; it's one experience. Without deliberate integration, you get three teams, three timelines, and a show where the pieces never quite land together.\n\nIntegration done well is mostly invisible infrastructure: clean signal flow, sensible network architecture, labeled racks, and control logic that lets an operator run a complex show from one surface. It's the difference between technology that supports the creative and technology that competes with it."

[services] services/av-system-integration (id 4)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[2].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "BEATROX integrates the full technical stack: system design and specification, signal flow and network architecture, rack builds and cable infrastructure, control programming across DMX, Art-Net, sACN, MIDI, and timecode, and on-site commissioning with calibration and testing. Because our designers, fabricators, and technicians work as one team, the AV is integrated into the scenic and structural design from day one — not threaded through it afterward.\n\nWe've unified complex systems for demanding clients. On The Great Escape for Adidas in Los Angeles, integrated AV drove a complete product launch experience, with audio, video, and interactive elements operating as a single environment. Projekt X for Adriatique's worldwide 'X Future' tour required deep integration inside a custom-fabricated aluminum stage element — DMX-addressable edge-lit LEDs recessed into the structure itself, pixel-mapped and laser-mapped, designed to fly in any orientation and reassemble identically in every city. And for BuzzFeed's NewFronts at Moynihan Station, we integrated media server playback, individually mapped projection orbs, and full lighting programming into one programmed show flow on a custom-built stage."

[services] services/av-equipment-sourcing-rentals (id 3)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "AV equipment sourcing is the process of specifying, renting, and integrating the technical systems your event depends on — audio, video, lighting, control, and playback — and Beatrox runs it from Portland, Oregon for productions nationwide. Most events don't own this gear; it's assembled per-show from rental houses, specialty vendors, and in-house inventory, then engineered into a single coherent system.\n\nThe sourcing decision matters more than most people realize. The wrong projector spec, an underpowered amp rack, or a mismatched video processor can compromise the entire experience — and you usually don't find out until load-in. Knowing which vendor carries which equipment, what it actually costs, and how it behaves in the field is a skill built over hundreds of shows.\n\nYou need professional sourcing whenever your event has real technical demands: large-format projection, LED video walls, concert-grade audio, show control, or anything that has to run flawlessly in front of an audience. A single accountable partner who designs the system and supplies the gear eliminates the finger-pointing that comes with split responsibility."
  AFTER:  "AV equipment sourcing is the process of specifying, renting, and integrating the technical systems your event depends on (audio, video, lighting, control, and playback) and Beatrox runs it from Portland, Oregon for productions nationwide. [services] services/3d-animation-motion-capture (id 1)  << status=published (RENDERS LIVE)
  field:  body[0].content
  method: exact-map
  BEFORE: "3D animation for live events is content that moves with purpose — characters, particles, simulations, and rendered worlds built by our Portland, Oregon team to play on screens, projections, and LED walls at productions nationwide. Motion capture takes it further: cameras and depth sensors track a real person's body and translate it into a digital performance in real time.\n\nYou need these tools whenever the experience should respond to the people inside it. An AR mirror that mirrors your movements, a flock of fish that scatters when you reach toward it, an avatar minted from your own body scan — these moments feel like magic to guests, and they're powered by motion capture pipelines and real-time 3D engines.\n\nThe difference between a gimmick and a memory is craft. Tracking has to be fast and stable, animation has to feel alive, and the system has to run flawlessly for hours of continuous foot traffic. That's the work we do."
  AFTER:  "3D animation for live events is content that moves with purpose: characters, particles, simulations, and rendered worlds built by our Portland, Oregon team to play on screens, projections, and LED walls at productions nationwide. Spatial audio recreated the immersive sounds of the inner forest canopy, and low-lying fog was used to create a sense of discovery and intrigue.\n\nThe project also utilized audio-reactive lighting, scenic elements hung from the roof, and custom signage to create a surreal scene to keep visitors entertained as they experienced the new Adidas jacket. The projector was short-throw and hidden within the forest canopy. The projection content was a slowly animating forest scene using forced perspective, making visitors feel like they were gently floating through a peaceful forest, even within a tiny Manhattan space."
